# Decisions, approvals and exceptions

Status key: **Approved** (Jared said so), **Applied – needs review** (done in the preview, flagged for Jared), **Proposed** (not applied).

## D-44 — Richmond / Tri-Cities gap analysis; board packages as the official numbers source; market data layer
- **Source files archived:** `docs/research/muse-competitor-gaps-richmond-tricities-2026-11-01.md`; GVR and FVREB September 2026 statistics packages in `docs/research/market-data/`.
- **Decision:** Richmond, Coquitlam, Port Coquitlam, Port Moody, Anmore and Belcarra pages follow the D-38 page template and are built in the Metro Vancouver pass, after the Fraser Valley and Surrey pages, at the same quality bar as the core market.
- **Market data layer:** `tools/parse_market.py` turns the two board PDFs (text-extracted) into `src/data/market/<YYYY-MM>.json`. `tools/apply_market.py` then rebuilds a generated, dated, sourced price section in every local-knowledge file that carries a `market_spec`. Generated blocks are tagged `gen: market` and replaced on each run, so a new month refreshes every page with one command.
- **Labelling rule:** board figures are community-level (all of Maple Ridge, not one neighbourhood). Sub-area guides show them as the city context and say so; they are never presented as sub-area statistics.
- **Boilerplate rule:** no shared explanatory sentence is repeated across pages. Only the numbers and one short lead-in are generated, because repeated sentences lower page uniqueness and add nothing for the reader.
- **Refresh:** October reports are expected about 4–5 November. Drop the new PDFs in, re-run both tools, review the diff, commit.
- **Status:** Maple Ridge, Pitt Meadows and Tsawwassen use GVR; Surrey, Langley, Abbotsford, Mission, White Rock and North Delta use FVREB; Chilliwack and the east valley (CADREB) publish board-wide figures only and need Paragon exports for community numbers.

## D-45 — Commercial and industrial market data; Fraser Valley commercial market report page
- **Sources received 2026-11-01:** Greater Vancouver REALTORS® Commercial Edge Q2 2026 report (news release dated October 8, 2026) and Avison Young's Metro Vancouver industrial market report Q3 2026.
- **Decision:** the facts (counts, dollars, vacancy, rents, construction) are stored as data in `src/data/market/commercial-gvr-2026-Q2.json` and `industrial-ay-2026-Q3.json`, each transcribed from the PDF tables and checked against the printed totals (area sales sum to 285/293/332; industrial rows sum to the Metro Vancouver row). Prose is original and computed from the data by `tools/build_commercial_page.py`; no report text, charts, tenant or buyer names are reproduced.
- **Page:** `/fraser-valley-commercial-market-report.html` (draft), linked from `/commercial.html`. Refreshed by dropping in new JSON files and re-running the script (next: GVR Q3 around January 2027, Avison Young Q4 around January 2027).
- **PDFs are not committed.** The Avison Young report is marked all rights reserved and the repo's privacy is still unconfirmed (B-25). Only extracted facts and the source citation are kept.
- **Published percentages are used where the source gives them** (for example office dollar volume +68.4%) rather than recomputed from rounded chart values.
- **Scope (Jared, 2026-11-01): commercial is wanted as a side mission, not the main thing, because he does receive commercial listings.** Residential work keeps priority. Commercial additions are limited to this report, its quarterly refresh and a few per-city industrial pages only when time allows.

## D-46 — Verified local facts from the town-facts file; Fraser Valley short-term rental and zoning rules page
- **Source:** "Town facts verification" file (2026-11-01), prepared for another project. Only the Fraser Valley facts were kept; they are in `docs/research/verified-local-facts-2026-11-01.md` with source URLs and unverified items.
- **Page:** `/fraser-valley-short-term-rental-and-suite-rules.html` (draft) covers Chilliwack, Abbotsford, Mission, Maple Ridge, White Rock and the Hope/Harrison provincial exemption. Only verified facts are stated; unverified items (Chilliwack Bylaw 5395 adoption date, Abbotsford SSMUH day and bylaw number, White Rock s.5.8 conditions) are named as unconfirmed on the page.
- **Maple Ridge:** Bylaw No. 7998-2024, adopted June 25, 2024, re-read by me on the City's own PDF and added to the Maple Ridge selling page.
- **Rule:** a bylaw date or number goes on a page only if an official page states it. Gaps stay visible instead of being filled from memory.
- **Next:** short-term rental and suite rules for Langley City, Langley Township, Surrey, Delta and Pitt Meadows have not been researched yet.

## D-47 — Presale data layer and presale pages (Muse list of 2026-11-01)
- **Source:** Muse's presale master list (134 projects completing 2027–2029; GVR 83, Fraser Valley 51) and info pack, built from public listings and developers' own pages, read 2026-11-01. Archived at `docs/research/presale-master-list-2026-11-01.csv`; the info pack's appendices (completion not stated; big projects not confirmed in the window) are not published.
- **Generator:** `tools/build_presale.py` writes `src/data/presale/<date>.json` and the pages. New list, new run.
- **Pricing rule:** a price is shown only when the source dates it within three months of the snapshot; otherwise the page says "Ask for current pricing". Ranges such as "the $400,000s" keep their wording, and bedroom qualifiers are kept. 15 of 134 projects pass today. Reason: several list prices are tagged to 2025 or "prior file", and a stale presale price on a public page misleads buyers.
- **Pages (all draft):** hub `/presale-projects-fraser-valley-and-metro-vancouver.html` (all 134, Fraser Valley first) and city pages for Surrey (25), Abbotsford (8), Burnaby (21), Coquitlam (21) and Vancouver (22). The existing Langley and South Surrey presale pages get a project table through the local layer; archive wording is untouched. Cities with under eight projects appear on the hub only.
- **Not on pages:** source URLs (several are other agents' sites), unsourced incentives, and developer or sales-centre claims. Deposit wording is shown as published.
- **Gaps:** no Chilliwack, Mission, White Rock or Hope projects on the list; Paragon presale data (via Jared) is still to be merged; neighbourhood per project is not in the sources.
- **Overlap to confirm with Jared:** `langleypresale.ca` is his separate Langley presale directory; the Langley table on jaredgibbons.ca overlaps it. Presale mechanics (tax, deposits, 7-day rescission) already exist on his Langley page and are linked, not repeated.

## D-48 — Specialty-search matrix; multiplex and suites page
- **Source:** Muse's specialty-search matrix (101 research files × 23 specialties) and notes, archived at `docs/research/muse-specialty-matrix-2026-11-01.csv` and `...-notes.md`. A cell is REAL only where the area's own research file documents it; I do not have those files, so I use the matrix for priorities and link targets, never for facts about an area.
- **Findings used:** multiplex/SSMUH potential is REAL in 50 areas, homes with suites in 48, carriage houses in 30, acreage in 29, transit anchors in 19; no dedicated pages exist. Pool, fixer-upper, pet-friendly strata and rentals-allowed strata have no evidence and get no pages (they are listing-level facts). Presale demand is REAL in 21 areas but UNVERIFIED in 19, so presale pages follow D-47 only.
- **Page:** `/homes-with-multiplex-potential-fraser-valley.html` (draft). Rules were read by me on official pages on 2026-11-01: Province, Surrey, Pitt Meadows, Delta, Langley City (bylaw 3284 and readings notice), plus the D-46 facts for Maple Ridge, Mission, Abbotsford and Chilliwack. Where a page gave no number or date, the cell says so. The neighbourhood link list uses the 21 matrix areas that already have a guide page.
- **Gaps:** Township of Langley and White Rock SSMUH rules; Delta bylaw numbers and adoption dates; Langley City adoption date; suite and coach house rules for most cities.
- **Backlog:** 25 areas with REAL multiplex potential have no page; listed in `docs/research/page-backlog-from-matrix-2026-11-01.md` and built under the correct parent.

## D-49 — Education and childcare inventory (schools layer)
- **Source:** Muse's education pack, archived at `docs/research/education-coverage-notes-2026-11-01.md` and `education-by-area-2026-11-01.md`. K-12 comes from the BC Ministry of Education and Child Care school list (930 rows in program cities), childcare from the Child Care Map registry (3,462 licensed facilities), post-secondary from the Province's locations file. The row-level `education-inventory.csv` (addresses, districts) was not supplied.
- **Finding:** the Muse area placement is not safe to publish as it stands. It rests on Muse's reading of research files, not on addresses; the category labels are unreliable (secondary schools listed as elementary: Kitsilano, Prince of Wales, Sir Winston Churchill, Kwantlen Park, Frank Hurt and others); one placement is plainly wrong (King George Secondary, a Vancouver school, filed under East Cambie, Richmond); 23 schools are claimed by two areas; only 3 of 3,462 childcare facilities were placed; and the archive's `downtown-vw` page is titled "Downtown West Vancouver" although VW is Vancouver West.
- **Rule applied (`tools/build_schools.py`):** tier 1 publishes a school only when it is named for its own neighbourhood (distinctive shared word, not a person's name or a wider place name) or is a post-secondary campus the notes call unambiguous. Level comes from the school's name, never from the rollup label. Tier 2 (120 placements) is staged in `src/data/schools/placements-2026-11-01.json` unpublished. Childcare is not published.
- **Pages:** 31 existing pages get a short "Schools in <area>" section (generated blocks tagged `gen: schools`, rebuilt each run), with a catchment caveat and the source line. Single namesake schools are worded as "one of the schools", never as the only school. No ratings or rankings.
- **Not done:** per-city school counts and school lists by city; they need the CSV. 24 placement keys belong to areas with no page yet (Port Kells, Kent, Hope, Agassiz, Mt Lehman, Aldergrove Langley, Brookswood Langley, Surrey City Centre and others) and will be used when those pages are built.

Quilchena was published in the first pass and is now removed.

- Neighbourhood sections now show each school's street address.

## D-51 — Boundaries, education v2 and Tsawwassen East
- **Boundaries (Muse, `docs/research/muse-boundaries-readme-2026-11-01.md`):** the board publishes no sub-area boundary text or file. They exist only as a member map layer in Paragon and AUTOPROP. Municipal neighbourhood boundaries are not substituted, because the two geographies differ: Sunshine Hills Woods combines two municipal neighbourhoods, Willoughby Heights is Willoughby to the Township, Surrey City Centre is the board's Whalley, and Mt Lehman is not a board sub-area at all. Board codes seen in public material: South Surrey/White Rock F51-F59, Langley F60-F69 and F6A, Abbotsford East F75.
- **Route for the 116 held school placements:** a lookup sheet, `docs/research/school-subarea-lookup-sheet-2026-11-01.csv`, with each school's address, postal code and coordinates and a blank column for the Paragon sub-area. Anyone with Paragon access can fill it by address. Until it comes back, those placements stay unpublished.
- **Education v2** (`education-inventory-v2-2026-11-01.csv`) is a strict superset of v1 (same 4,460 rows, no changed values) and adds latitude, longitude, postal code and French Immersion, Francophone, alternate and online flags. The city list pages now use the flags for counts and an "other public schools and programs" group, and show a French Immersion column. The flags agree with Jared's catchment guide for the South Surrey feeder schools; the Ministry file also marks Pacific Heights Elementary, which the guide does not mention, so the page says to ask the district.
- **Tsawwassen East guide** (draft, 0.70 unique) written from Muse's verified file plus the City of Delta development page, which I read myself. Left out on purpose: the 4,000 m2 RD2 lot size (looks wrong for Beach Grove; I could not open Delta's zoning sheet), the "sample address" school result (570 Boundary Bay Road is the regional park, not a home), Diefenbaker Park (its source was another page), and tax due dates. The SD3 floor, land and setback figures are published with attribution but are marked in the file as needing a check against the OCP text.

The shared rules page now has a Pitt Meadows section.

## D-55 — Inventory gate (5 active listings) from Jared's Paragon count, 2026-11-01
- **Input:** Jared's briefing and sheet (`docs/research/paragon-inventory-briefing-2026-11-01.md`). Rule: an inventory page needs at least 5 active listings. 14 of 638 pages are under 5; 1,138 sub-area and search pairs have 5+ listings and no page.
- **Decision (mine, reversible):** the 10 under-5 pages that are only a listing feed (Annacis Island, Tilbury, Lake City Industrial, Oakdale, Braemar, North Arm, Capilano NV, Delbrook, Big Bend, Greentree Village) are set to `noindex, follow` and left out of the sitemap; URLs stay reachable and nothing is redirected. The four under-5 pages that carry substantial original text (Westham Island guide, North Maple Ridge guide, West Meadows guide, Tsawwassen farms) stay indexed and are flagged for review, because their value is the guide, not the feed. Annacis Island and Tilbury have text but zero residential listings, so they are gated.
- **How:** `src/data/inventory/gate.json` (counts, action, note per slug), read by `src/lib/inventory.ts`. The sitemap skips gated slugs. `Base.astro` now takes a per-page `robots` value but still emits `noindex, nofollow` on every page unless the build sets `PUBLIC_PREVIEW=false`, so the preview stays closed and **launch remains a separate step needing Jared's approval**. Expired own listings and the 404 page are noindex in launch mode too.
- **Tests:** `tests/inventory.test.mjs` (3 tests; 31 in total) checks the gate against real pages, the sitemap, and a launch-mode build in a temporary folder.
- **Monthly:** rerun the Paragon counts, update `gate.json`; a page that reaches 5 is flipped to `keep` and returns to the sitemap.
- **Not done yet:** the briefing's 93 pages with no count (Chilliwack and city-wide pages) and the Gaps tab; I could read only the first tab of the sheet. Fraser Valley gaps come first in the build: Fleetwood Tynehead, White Rock, Abbotsford West (detached), Mission BC (attached and acreage), Brookswood, Campbell Valley, Salmon River and Elgin Chantrell (acreage, coach houses), King George Corridor, Aldergrove and Dewdney Deroche (manufactured). Greater Vancouver attached gaps (Brighouse, Metrotown, West End, Lower Lonsdale, Mount Pleasant) are built last at the same quality, as before.

## D-56 — "Every page over 70% unique" (Jared, 2026-11-01)
- **Rule change:** the 5-listing rule became a rolling 3-month average (one day's count never gates a page); `gate.json` now stores monthly history per page. New pages are also held out of the index and the sitemap while their uniqueness is under 0.70 (`src/data/inventory/uniqueness.json`, written by `python3 -I tools/uniqueness.py --write-gate`). Archive pages are never auto-held, so no archived URL drops out silently.
- **Measurement fixes:** `tools/uniqueness.py` now measures brand-new pages too (it ignored them before, so the selling, schools, rules and presale pages had never been scored) and treats a list that is only links as navigation, not body text. Both changes are in the tool's docstring. Result: all 58 new pages are at 0.70 or above (lowest 0.698).
- **Raised to 0.70+:** 18 school list pages (shared boilerplate paragraphs replaced by computed, city-specific sentences naming schools, communities and French Immersion schools); presale hub (full tables now only on the pages that have no page of their own, plus a summary table) and city presale pages.
- **New:** 22 market-stats pages (`<city>-real-estate-market-stats`, generated monthly by `tools/build_market_pages.py` from the board's HPI and FVREB summary; every sentence is computed from that month's numbers). Linked from the city hubs.
- **Not raised, and why:** 310 of 708 pages are still under 0.70 (49 under 300 words, 37 mid, 224 long). Nearly all are archive pages that repeat the same template text as their siblings (condos, townhomes, houses, 55+ pages for one city) or are neighbourhood pages of 50 to 300 words. I built and measured a data layer for the city and type pages from board HPI alone (`tools/build_type_layers.py`); it moved Surrey condos from 0.09 to 0.42 and Mission homes from 0.06 to 0.57, but it cannot reach 0.70, because the market-stats page already uses the same numbers. So I did not apply it, and no archived wording has been suppressed. Reaching 0.70 on those pages needs facts I do not have: Paragon sold history and days on market by area and sub-area, the sub-area active counts, first-hand notes from Jared, and neighbourhood facts from municipal sources.
- **Update (Jared, 2026-11-01 13:21):** pages under 70% are reviewed with Jared first, and nothing is taken out of the sitemap before that review. The uniqueness gate is therefore switched off (`enforce: false` in `src/data/inventory/uniqueness.json`; the switch is one line). The review list is `docs/review/pages-under-70-percent-2026-11-01.csv` (310 pages: page type, URL, words, unique share, most overlapping page, what to add).

## D-57 — SnapStats monthly reports used for community and city market sections (Jared, 2026-11-01)
- **Input:** `SnapStats-2026.zip` from Jared, eight PDFs (GVR and FVR, June to September 2026). **Jared confirmed (13:30 and 13:31) that the figures may be used on the site, but the reports themselves may not be posted.** So only numbers are used, in our own tables and wording, with a source line; no report page, chart or text is copied and the PDFs are not in the repo.
- **Coverage:** 14 areas: Burnaby, New Westminster, Coquitlam, Port Coquitlam, Port Moody, Pitt Meadows, Maple Ridge (GVR) and Surrey, South Surrey / White Rock, North Delta, Cloverdale, Langley, Abbotsford, Mission (FVR). Not covered: Richmond, Vancouver, North Shore, Delta (Tsawwassen, Ladner), Chilliwack, Sardis, Squamish and Whistler.
- **Parser:** `tools/parse_snapstats.py` reads each page (price bands, bedrooms, communities, six summary figures, 13-month trend). Output is `src/data/snapstats/YYYY-MM.json`. Check: for all 112 pages (4 months x 14 areas x 2 types) the community, price-band and bedroom rows add up to the report's own totals, and the last point of each trend equals the month's totals.
- **Generator:** `tools/build_snapstats_layers.py` writes `gen: "snapstats"` blocks into `src/data/local/<slug>.json`. Rerun it, then `tools/uniqueness.py`, each month when new PDFs arrive.
  - 179 neighbourhood pages and guides (110 communities): the area page gets monthly listed, sold and sales ratio for houses and for condos and townhomes; the guide gets the community against its whole city (share of listings and sales, ratio against the city, rank, listing change, best-bet mentions). Different cuts, so the two pages do not repeat each other. Communities with no listings or sales in all four months get no section.
  - 34 city and home-type pages (houses, homes, condos, townhomes in 13 cities): each city-wide table (price bands, bedrooms, monthly sale price and days on market, 13-month trend) is used on exactly one page per city.
- **Wording choices:** the reports show one "Sale Price" a month without saying average or median, so the site says "reported sale price" and says so under the table. Market type uses the reports' own scale (under 12% buyers, 12 to 20% balanced, over 20% sellers). Caveats sit in the table source note.
- **Effect on uniqueness (measured):** of the 310 pages under 70%, 137 got a section; the average share on those moved 0.51 to 0.54 and one page (Pitt Meadows homes) crossed 0.70. The reason is the sibling-template text in the archive pages; the data adds real information but cannot outweigh 1,000+ words of repeated copy. Surrey houses, condos and townhomes (0.09) rose to about 0.30. List with before and after: `docs/review/pages-under-70-percent-after-snapstats-2026-11-01.csv`.
- **Needs Jared's decision:** to reach 0.70 on the template-heavy pages the repeated archive sections have to be replaced or rewritten (`suppress` on the shared sections). Not done: it changes archived wording.
- **Gaps found:** 60 communities in the reports have no page (all Metrotown, Central Park, Highgate and other Burnaby areas, Coquitlam's Westwood Plateau, Maillardville and North Coquitlam, Port Moody Centre, Anmore, Belcarra, Port Coquitlam's Citadel and Glenwood, New West's Sapperton and Uptown, South Surrey's Grandview and Sunnyside Park, Port Kells). These are the next pages to build.
- **Not matched yet:** the four North Surrey pages (their community list is not confirmed), and any home type the reports do not split (duplexes, penthouses, waterfront).
- **Heads-up:** the reports' cover shows Royal LePage Northstar Realty, 118 - 15272 Croydon Drive. Jared's brokerage on the site is Little Oak Realty (Suite 201). Nothing from the cover is used.

## D-58 — 48 community pages, link minimum, review variety (Jared, 2026-11-01 13:41)
- **Jared's instruction:** "Yes build the 60 missing community pages", and asked whether the 137 updated pages have a minimum number of links to other pages and whether the reviews vary.
- **The 60:** 9 already had a page under another slug (Metrotown, Central, Central Park, Forest Glen, Forest Hills, Fraserview, Anmore, Grandview, Sunnyside Park); they got the data sections (aliases in `tools/build_snapstats_layers.py`). 48 are new draft pages (`tools/build_community_pages.py`, status `draft`, slug `<community>-<city>`, in the sitemap). 3 were not built because nothing was listed or sold there in any of the four months: Westwood Summit, Brunette, Ioco.
- **What a new page has:** title and meta with the community and city, H1, monthly listings and sales for the home types that had activity, share of the city by month, the community against its city, 4 to 6 FAQ answers computed from the same figures (FAQPage markup), a list of 12+ links (city hub, market stats, home-type pages, sell page, schools list, ten neighbouring communities), reviews. Names follow the reports; "Summitt View" (Coquitlam) is spelled as the reports spell it and needs Jared or Muse to confirm.
- **Uniqueness (measured):** median 0.64, range 0.56 to 0.75; 6 of 48 are at 0.70 or more. Small communities have little to say from the figures alone (some had two homes listed), so the numbers repeat across pages. Each page lists what it still needs (street boundaries, per-community sold prices and days on market from Paragon, schools by address, Jared's notes, a listing feed). `docs/review/new-community-pages-2026-11-01.csv`.
- **Link audit (before):** among the 137 pages with a new section, 36 had fewer than 10 internal links in the main content (some had 1, the listing-feed pages) and 39 had no inbound link from another page's main content. **Now:** every community page and guide (237) has at least 12 contextual links, and every community page has at least 10 inbound links; every city hub has a "neighbourhoods" list (`tools/link_communities.py`, `pos: "end"` blocks placed after the archive content).
- **Review audit (before):** the archive shows one set of six Google excerpts on 200 of 251 pages (two other sets on 43 and 8), and none on the other 399 pages. **Now:** `src/lib/reviews.ts` shows 3 per page from the pool of all 11 archived excerpts (seeded by the page's slug; selling-side excerpts pinned to selling, condo and townhome pages, buyer-side to home pages). 357 pages show reviews (the 106 new pages included) with 171 different combinations; the most common appears on 16 pages. Wording is untouched. **Only 11 excerpts exist in the archive.** More real reviews (Google, with permission; initials only) would widen it. No review markup (schema) is emitted: it is not eligible for a business's reviews of itself.
- **Open:** a few archive pages jump from H1 to H3 (axe heading-order, moderate), for example Burnaby and North Delta homes pages. It is in the archived text, not the new blocks.

## D-59 — Muse keyword plan and specialty matrix, expanded versions (Jared, 2026-11-01 14:16)
- **Input:** two files from Jared with no instruction: `keywords.csv` (458 rows; the version in D-37 had 78) and `specialty-matrix.csv` (481 rows; was 101). They replace the copies in `docs/research/`. Muse still marks every search volume UNVERIFIED (Keyword Planner pull pending), so no page quotes a volume.

Of the 123: 87 differ only in phrase (for example "homes for sale" wording), 18 lack the area or place wording, 13 lack the home type, 5 are not area pages or conflict (see below).

The preview shows the "listings not connected" notice. 12 of the 48 have a Muse row (Citadel, Glenwood, College Park, Heritage Woods, North Shore Port Moody and others); the rest follow the same pattern.

The largest groups: multiplex potential 186, homes with suites 174, coach houses 110, acreage 109, transit anchors 68. These need Paragon counts per sub-area and search (the Gaps tab) before a page is justified.

## D-60 — Keyword titles applied to 27 of the 31 area-name and home-type rows (Jared, 2026-11-01 14:42)
- **Approval:** Jared said "go ahead with the 31", then asked that each title use the name with the most traffic and best SEO. Muse's volumes are still unverified and there is no Search Console export, so "most traffic" means how the place or product is normally searched, not a measured figure. No page quotes a volume.
- **Where I did not copy Muse:** "Downtown VW", "Fraserview NW" and "Gst Rebate New Home Bc" are not how people search, so they were not used. Downtown VW became "Downtown Vancouver Condos for Sale". Arbutus became "Arbutus Ridge" because that is the neighbourhood name. Tight pages use "Jared Gibbons" without REALTOR® to stay at 66 characters or fewer.
- **Mechanism:** `src/data/seo-overrides.json` (written by `tools/build_seo_overrides.py`) is applied over the archive record in `src/pages/[...slug].astro`. The archive JSON is untouched, so deleting an entry restores the archived title, H1 and description. 27 pages changed; 15 also got a matching H1 and/or meta description so the three agree.
- **Held, 4 pages:** `fraserview-nw` (Muse's "NW" is an abbreviation), `downtown-ve` (Muse's keyword is the downtown-vw page, so changing both would make two pages compete), `gst-rebate-in-bc` (current title already carries the first-time-buyer wording), `privacy` (not a search page).
- **Check:** build 1,163 pages; each of the 27 pages has the new title, one H1 and the matching H1 text; tests 32 pass. File: `docs/review/keyword-titles-applied-2026-11-01.csv`.
- **Still waiting:** 87 phrase-only title rows and 5 city-conflict rows.

- **Direction:** "Muse is just good for information, if things don't make sense then don't use it." Every Muse keyword is now checked against how the place is searched and against the pages already on the site before it is used.

## D-62 — Review pool grown from 11 to 78 excerpts, matched to page topic (Jared, 2026-11-01 14:52)
- **Input:** `Jared-Gibbons-5-Star-Google-Reviews.pdf` (94 five-star Google reviews pulled Nov 1, 2026; initials only), saved at `docs/research/jared-google-5-star-reviews-2026-11-01.pdf`. Jared asked whether to use different ones throughout.
- **Answer:** yes. `tools/build_review_pool.py` turns the export into `src/data/review-excerpts.json` (78 excerpts, each a run of whole sentences, verbatim, cut with an ellipsis where long). Skipped: 5 rating-only entries, reviews about a death in the family or a hard period in someone's life, and any sentence naming a team member or the firm. Initials only, no dates beyond the 8 archive excerpts that already carried one, no Review structured data (D-22).

- **Result across 362 pages that show reviews:** 323 different combinations of three; the most-shown excerpt appears on 76 pages (21%), against one set of six on 200 pages in the archive.
- **Commercial pages (Jared, 2026-11-01 16:35: "Use the residential reviews for the commercial pages as well"):** `commercial`, `lake-city-industrial`, `tilbury`, `annacis-island`, `west-chilliwack` and the commercial market report now show three excerpts: the two commercial-lease reviews (C.E., S.H.) first, then one residential review.

The 167 community pages now link to their type pages. List: `docs/review/type-pages-2026-11-01.csv`.

They are drafts for review, not for launch as they stand; the gate stays off so nothing leaves the sitemap before Jared sees the list. Existing pages moved by an average of 0.001; 24 pages dropped by more than 0.02, and the 48 generated community pages went from a median of 0.641 to 0.619 because of the added link lists.

## D-64 — Every content page has an FAQ, at least 5 links, and its own set of three reviews (Jared, 2026-11-01 17:02)
- **Direction:** "Make sure all of them have faq section, minimum 5 links and different reviews." Audit: `python3 -I tools/audit_pages.py` (reads `dist/`); per-page result in `docs/review/page-audit-faq-links-reviews-2026-11-01.csv`. Before: 647 of 966 pages had no FAQ, 125 had fewer than 5 links in the main content, 394 had no reviews. After: every content page has an FAQ, 5+ links and reviews; the only exceptions are utility pages (404, privacy, contact, subscribe, reviews, testimonials, blog) and the bespoke home and Fort Langley pages, which keep their own archived content.
- **FAQ (`tools/build_muse_faq.py`):** every content page gets at least 4 questions from four sources, in this order: (1) Muse's workbook tab "7 FAQs" (gen:"muse"), only where the answer has no UNVERIFIED / cannot / not-confirmed wording and is under 700 characters; an answer built on a monthly market number is kept only if it says "as of <Month> 20xx", so those will go stale each month and are refreshed by re-running the script; (2) computed SnapStats questions (gen:"snap-faq": listing counts, buyers/sellers read, change since June, share of city, reported sale price, days on market); (3) home-type questions matched to the page (gen:"bank-faq": condo, townhome, house, duplex, 55+, mobile, penthouse, farm, acreage, foreclosure, guide), only where the page really is that type; (4) two plain showing / home-evaluation questions (gen:"muse-generic") only to fill. Place names are cleaned (stop words, repeated words). Source links Muse put in brackets move to a "Sources" list at the end. A question repeated on an area page and its guide is used once. Exempt (generated FAQs stripped): testimonials, 404, privacy, subscribe, reviews, home, blog, contact, unsubscribe, terms, accessibility; About has a hand-written five-question FAQ about Jared, his office and contact details (taken from the archive). Muse answers carry her citations (read 2026-11-01); I spot-checked a handful but have not verified each, which is why they are labelled for one-pass review or removal.
- **Links (`tools/link_pages.py`):** 121 pages got a "More real estate nearby" list (region hub, market stats, selling page, schools, type pages and neighbours in the same region, ring order so neighbours also gain inbound links). Pages with no region (about, marketing plan, map search) get the main hubs.
- **Reviews (`tools/assign_reviews.py`):** 961 pages each show three excerpts, and no two pages show the same three; no excerpt is on more than 12% of pages. Matching by page topic is as in D-62. Archive pages that had no review block now have one, placed before the end-of-page link lists.
- **Uniqueness:** median 0.649; pages at 0.70 or more 394. Very short pages (about 50 words) look like 1.0 before and 0.78 after because they now hold real content.
- **Monthly order:** build_snapstats_layers, build_community_pages, build_snapstats_layers, build_type_pages, link_communities, build_muse_faq, `npm run build`, link_pages, `npm run build`, assign_reviews, `npm run build`, uniqueness.

## D-67 — Missing home-type pages filled where Jared's Paragon count is 5 or more (Jared, 2026-11-01 17:59)
- **Rule:** a page is built only if Jared's Paragon active count for that city and type is 5+ (docs/research/paragon-inventory-briefing-2026-11-01.md, "By Area" tab, saved in `tools/data/paragon-by-area-2026-11-01.json`) and the site had no page. Counts are a one-day snapshot and are not published; they sit in `src/data/inventory/gate.json` so the rolling 3-month rule applies. Attached counts (condos and townhomes) and "Retirement Community" for 55+ are the nearest Paragon measures, not exact.
- **Built (32 after consolidation, `tools/build_gap_pages.py`):** acreages for Surrey, South Surrey, Cloverdale, Mission, Ladner, Burnaby, Coquitlam (7); lots and land for North Delta, Burnaby, Coquitlam, Port Coquitlam, Port Moody, New Westminster (6); one 55+ page per city for Surrey, Burnaby, Coquitlam, Port Coquitlam (4; the condo/townhome twins were merged because the reports cannot split them); Coquitlam mobile homes (1); condos and townhomes pages for North Delta, Mission, Burnaby, Coquitlam, Port Coquitlam, Port Moody, New Westminster (14). The earlier count of 36 included the four 55+ twins.
- **Not built (no Paragon count or under 5):** duplexes, penthouses, waterfront, equestrian (count columns empty); mobile homes in Cloverdale, Delta, Ladner, Tsawwassen, Pitt Meadows, Burnaby (0); Chilliwack (CADREB not searched); White Rock (not separable from South Surrey in Paragon areas); Richmond, Vancouver, the North Shore (outside the Fraser Valley focus, no SnapStats lines).
- **Content (rewritten 2026-11-01):** each page gets data that is different for its type, so twins do not repeat: condos use the board's apartment benchmark and apartment sales/listings, townhomes use the townhouse benchmark and the gap to other types, acreages use detached benchmark and detached communities, lots use composite benchmark and detached monthly figures, 55+ and mobile use SnapStats slices. Each page also lists schools, child care or post-secondary from the BC Data Catalogue, sliced by category by page type (condos secondary schools, townhomes elementary and middle, acreages independent schools, lots and 55+ colleges and universities, mobile homes child care count). Own FAQ (5+), links, hand_edited.
- **Uniqueness after the rewrite:** 0.21 to 0.65 (weakest: 55+ pages and lots/land at 0.2 to 0.4, because the data for those types is thin). All are drafts under the 70% rule and need Jared's review; the uniqueness gate is `enforce:false`, so they enter the sitemap at launch unless it is switched on for new pages. Recommendation: enable `enforce` for pages under 0.4 until Jared supplies local notes.

## D-68 — Thin-trend rule, Data & methodology page and net-proceeds calculator (Jared, 2026-11-01)
- **Thin-trend rule (`tools/trend.py`):** a % change under 1.5% is "little changed" with the figure shown; a count-based change is never called a rise or fall when either count is under 3. All generators (market stats, communities, type pages, Brentwood, condo pages, gap pages, Muse FAQ answers, SnapStats city tables) call the same helpers. `tests/data-rules.test.mjs` scans generated text for violations. Also fixed: `build_type_pages` skipped regenerating houses pages when a condos page existed, leaving stale text; fixed.
- **Data & methodology page (`/data-and-methodology.html`, `tools/build_methodology_page.py`):** sources with the month/release read, definitions, the two wording rules, what is not published; linked from the footer on every page. Every sourced table now ends with a link to it. Draft: Jared should confirm the list of sources and wording.
- **Net-proceeds calculator (`/seller-net-proceeds-calculator.html`, `NetProceedsCalc.astro`):** runs in the browser only (no server, nothing stored or sent). No default rates: sale price, payout, commission %, tax on commission %, legal, penalty, adjustments and other costs are all typed in by the visitor. Jared has not supplied his commission, legal or closing costs, so none are published (`needs_from_muse`). Linked from home-evaluation, selling and the sellers FAQ.
- **Inline definitions:** table source lines carry the source, the month and the definition; the methodology link is added to each. Sentence-level definitions for terms such as sales ratio and benchmark are in the same source line, not repeated in every sentence.


## D-69 — Paragon sold export added as a grouped-figures layer (Jared, 2026-11-01 19:15)
- **Input:** `Paragon_Sold_Residential_2025-11-01_to_2026-10-31` (CSV and the same data as XLSX). 33,974 rows: 30,250 Closed and 3,724 Pending. Only Closed rows are used, because a Pending row's "sold" price is its list price. The last closed sale is October 22, 2026, so the window reads "November 1, 2025 to October 22, 2026".
- **What is stored:** `src/data/sold/sold-12m.json` holds grouped figures only (per sub-area and per city; per home type; complexes with 3+ sales). The raw file carries MLS numbers, so it is NOT in the repo; re-aggregate with `python3 -I tools/build_sold_layer.py --csv <file>`.
- **Rules (same spirit as the 1.5% / 3 rule, D-68):** a median is published only for 5+ sales; a count is mentioned for 3+; a building is named with a price only with 5+ sales; a quarter-by-quarter table needs 5+ in every quarter and is never described as a trend. No address, MLS number or single sale price is published.
- **Where it shows:** 238 community and home-type pages (houses / condos / townhomes / duplexes split, with buildings tables on condo pages), the 32 gap pages (city level: condos, townhomes, acreages, lots and land, manufactured homes), and the four Brentwood Park pages. Brentwood Park now has a true split (490 condos, 14 townhomes, 20 houses sold in the window) instead of the combined SnapStats line.
- **Not covered:** 55+ pages (the export has no age-restriction field, so there is no 55+ sold data); lots and land and acreages in cities where fewer than 5 sold (pages say so and do not quote a typical price).
- **Gap-page uniqueness after this change:** about 0.33 to 0.66 (55+ pages 0.21 to 0.44). Still under the 70% rule: the added content is numeric and page-specific, but the sentence frames repeat across the 32 pages. Raising them further needs Jared's local notes (streets, buildings, what buyers ask).
- **Monthly order addition:** run `build_sold_layer.py` after `build_muse_faq.py` (it appends sold FAQ entries) and before the final builds.

- **Input:** 59 batch zips plus the batch 18 lots-and-land zip: 1,397 notes (441 condos, 503 townhomes, 450 lots and land) plus 5 more 55+ notes, saved in `docs/research/local-notes-types/` and `docs/research/local-notes-55plus/`. Batches 2 and 5 of the 55+ set are still missing.

## D-73 — Lots-and-land batches 19 to 21, wrong contact email fixed, text scrub (2026-11-02)
- **Input:** lots-and-land batches 19, 20 and 21 (53 notes) and the lots-and-land program summary (`docs/research/local-notes-types/lots-and-land-PROGRAM-SUMMARY.md`). All 503 lots-and-land notes are now in. 609 notes are used on 247 pages.

The archive record is untouched (reversible by removing one line). 0 pages in the build carry the wrong address.

It reached 17 pages before the scrub. `build_55plus_notes.clean` and the FAQ filter now drop it, and a test fails the build if any returns.

## D-76 — MLS 55+ flag from the closed-sales remarks export (2026-11-02)
- **Received:** `Paragon_Closed_Sales_Remarks_2025-11-01_to_2026-10-31.csv` (16,972 closed sales, 34 columns incl. Restricted Age, Title to Land, Public Remarks) and a zip of the same rows split by area (identical totals and columns). The export is not stored in the repo (listing remarks and MLS numbers); `tools/build_mls55.py` keeps only an aggregate at `docs/research/mls-55plus/flagged-buildings.json`.
- **What it showed:** 598 sales carry Restricted Age 55+. A building counts as flagged when at least half of its sales in the export carry the flag; 242 buildings pass, 6 do not (a flag on a minority of sales is noise from a neighbourhood-sized name). 140 passing buildings were not on any 55+ page.
- **Cross-check of our own calls:** the buildings the notes found NOT 55+ show no flag on any sale (Breakaway Bays, The Burlington, Burlington Square, Abbey Road, Wildwood Park, Central Park House, Greentree Village, The Loop). That settles the Breakaway Bays conflict in the Surrey note in favour of "no age restriction" from the MLS side. The Crescent and Burlington Estates are flagged, as the notes say.
- **Published:** a name-only table (building, type, title) on the 55+ page for the building's community, or on the city's 55+ condo/townhome page by community where the community has no page of its own. No counts, prices, addresses, MLS numbers or remarks. Buildings already described on a 55+ page are not repeated. The hub page explains that the flag is agent-typed and a lead, not proof (`#mls-flag`).
- **Held out:** Highgrove II (flagged on every sale, but the notes found no age restriction at Highgrove, 15055 20 Ave; needs a check whether they are separate stratas) and Queen's Park Place (Fraserview, New Westminster; no 55+ page for that area).
- **Monthly order:** `build_mls55.py <export.csv>` after `build_55plus_notes.py` and `build_typenotes.py`, before `npm run build`; without the argument it reapplies the saved list.
- **Not done:** the Public Remarks themselves are not published or quoted (listing agents' copy). Remarks mentioning 55+ on unflagged sales were not used.

It shows low-high ranges per area with the number of firms, each firm's own figures, Peak Law's all-in table, and the other published figures (Spagnuolo's budget, Zancope's "from", Pacific Point's general BC range) kept out of the fee tables because their basis differs. ## D-78 — Building facts in the MLS 55+ tables; how the Paragon remarks are used (2026-11-02)
- **Jared's direction:** the Paragon remarks export is realtor remarks to take wording or building wording from.
- **How it is used:** the listing agents' remarks are their own copy, so none is quoted or pasted. Building facts are extracted and written fresh: year built (only where a building's sales agree within two years), storeys (condos), and building amenities. Amenities come from the structured Amenities field, kept only where at least half of the building's flagged sales list them (that drops unit-level items like in-suite laundry); library, gated and RV parking come from the listing text as a yes/no. The 55+ tables now read Building, Type, Title, Built, Storeys, Building amenities. No counts, prices, addresses or MLS numbers.

The Cultus Lake sub-areas share one page.

- **Also fixed:** the three BC rules pages no longer carry a "no East Cambie-specific position is documented" paragraph that had leaked in.

## D-81 — October 2026 SnapStats added; research wording scrubbed from every page (2026-11-02)
- **Input:** `FVR_2026_October.pdf` and `GVR_2026_October.pdf` (SnapStats, data captured Nov 1, 2026). Same terms as D-57: figures only, the PDFs are not in the repo. `src/data/snapstats/2026-10.json` (14 areas, 28 reports, 340 community rows).
- **Check:** every report's price bands, bedrooms and communities add to its own total, each trend ends on the month's figures, and October's "September" column equals September's figures for all 28 reports. One exception, in the source: SnapStats' Mission detached price-band rows sum to 214 while its total, bedrooms and communities say 216. The parse matches the PDF; nothing is changed.
- **Effect:** the area, guide, type and city pages now show a Jun to Oct window and October as the current month. Pages built from the figures: 1 new type page (Matsqui, Abbotsford houses). Existing type pages that dipped under the 5-listing threshold in October are kept and refreshed instead of being left on September text (`build_type_pages.py`).
- **Bug fixed (`build_muse_faq.py`):** a page was skipped whenever no new FAQ was added, so FAQs computed from an earlier month ("from June to September 2026") were never cleared. It now rewrites when the list changes. Muse questions that quote one month's figures are dropped (we compute those).
- **Leak found and fixed (mine, from earlier batches):** 411 pages carried research-process wording: "UNCONFIRMED" / "UNVERIFIED" tags, "none exist in Jared's reports", "in this research", sibling-file and batch references, "locator result", "SnapStats FVR September 2026 report." as a whole answer. The earlier guards only covered some layers. New `tools/scrub_research_wording.py` runs last and: turns the tags into plain "not confirmed" / "not verified"; rewrites "(as of September 2026, Source: SnapStats …)" to "(SnapStats, September 2026)"; drops sentences about the research itself; drops a FAQ left under 40 characters or under 40% of a long answer; drops headings left empty. It also runs inside `build_55plus_notes.clean` (used by every notes builder) and `build_muse_faq`. Result: 3,650 tags rewritten, about 320 sentences, 164 FAQs and 37 list items removed, 93 citations shortened. Pages left under 4 FAQs are refilled by `build_muse_faq`.
- **Test:** new site-wide guard. Counts after: 48 tests pass.
- **Uniqueness:** 237 of 429 new pages under 0.70 (was 235 of 428; one new page and a little less text after the scrub).
- **Monthly order (full):** `parse_snapstats.py <dir>` → `build_snapstats_layers`, `build_community_pages`, `build_snapstats_layers`, `build_type_pages`, `build_condo_pages`, `build_brentwood`, `build_gap_pages`, `build_poco_55plus`, `link_communities`, `build_muse_faq`, `build_sold_layer`, `build_55plus_notes`, `build_typenotes`, `build_acreages`, `build_mobilehomes`, `build_duplex`, `build_nearby`, `build_nearby_buildings`, `build_mls55` (no argument), `build_muse_faq`, **`scrub_research_wording`**, `build_sold_layer`, then `npm run build`, `link_pages`, build, `assign_reviews`, build, `uniqueness.py --write-gate`, build, `npm test`. Do not run `build_typenotes` again after `build_acreages` (it strips the acreage, mobile-home and nearby sections).
- **Not updated:** the board HPI market pages (`src/data/market/2026-09.json`) stay on September; October HPI needs the board packages.

## D-100 — Mortgage and property transfer tax calculator (Jared, 2026-11-04)
- **Where:** `/mortgage-calculator.html` (the stripped archive form labels are replaced by the working calculator) and every own-listing page that has a numeric price (201 of 203; the two leased listings show a monthly rent, so they get none). The price is prefilled; sold listings read "Estimate a payment at this price".
- **Built:** `src/components/MortgageCalc.astro`, maths in `src/lib/mortgage.js`, 6 tests in `tests/mortgage.test.mjs` using the worked examples on the gov.bc.ca pages.
- **No interest rate is built in** (same rule as D-68). The visitor types the rate their lender quoted; the payment appears once they do. Default down payment is 20% and amortization 25 years; both are editable. Say if you want a rate prefilled and I will add it with a "typical, not a quote" label.
- **Sources checked 2026-11-04:** BC property transfer tax (1% to $200,000, 2% to $2M, 3% above, a further 2% over $3M residential), first-time buyer exemption (full to $500,000, $8,000 to $835,000, phased out at $860,000, registrations on or after April 1, 2024), newly built home exemption (full to $1.1M, phased out at $1.15M), CMHC premium by loan-to-value (0.60%, 1.70%, 2.40%, 2.80%, 3.10%, 4.00%). Fixed rates compound semi-annually.
- **Exemptions** are a selector (none, first-time buyer, newly built) and assume every buyer on title qualifies; the page says so. Foreign buyer tax, GST on new homes, legal fees, strata fees and property tax are listed as not included.
- **Runs in the browser only;** nothing is sent or stored, so no form-security work applies.
- **Not done on purpose:** the 25-year insured amortization surcharge is not on the CMHC page I read, so the calculator shows a notice instead of a number.

## D-101 — Bedroom pages: 24 built, 12 held (Jared, 2026-11-04)
- **Rule you set:** condos get 1, 2 and 3 bedroom pages, townhomes get 2 bedroom pages, no 1 bedroom townhome pages. I also left out 3 bedroom townhome pages: they are most of the townhome market and the city townhome page already covers them.
- **Areas:** Surrey (Surrey and North Surrey board areas), South Surrey, White Rock (added), Cloverdale, North Delta, Ladner, Tsawwassen, Langley, Abbotsford, Chilliwack (Chilliwack and Sardis board areas). Slugs read like `langley-2-bedroom-condos-for-sale`.
- **Threshold:** a page is built only where 30 or more sold in the past year. At 5 sales I built all 36 combinations, and every one under about 27 sales scored below the 70% unique line (not enough sales to fill neighbourhood and building tables), so those 12 are held, not published. They are in `docs/review/bedroom-pages-held.csv` (Abbotsford 3 bed 26, North Delta 2 bed 25, Tsawwassen 1 bed 22, and so on). Nothing was removed from any sitemap; they were never added. Tell me if you want any of them published anyway.
- **Data:** `src/data/sold/sold-beds.json` from `tools/build_bedroom_layer.py` (closed sales to October 22, grouped figures only; studios excluded; "3 bedroom" means 3 or more). Pages built by `tools/build_bedroom_pages.py`, now in `tools/chain.sh`.
- **Each page:** live-search placeholder, measures table, quarters, neighbourhoods and the buildings behind them, bedroom and nearby-city comparison, size and building-age tables, price ranges, mortgage calculator at the median price, FAQ, links. No live listing counts are published.
- **Live search:** each page needs a bedroom-filtered MyRealPage search. Worksheet for Jerry: `docs/review/idx/bedroom-searches-2026-11-04.xlsx` (24 rows). Until he sends IDs, each page shows the same "not connected" notice as the other pages.
- **Links in:** each city condo and townhome page has a "by number of bedrooms" list, and the explore block lists the bedroom pages ("Langley 2 bedroom condos").
- **Uniqueness:** 24 of 24 above 70% (lowest 71.5%).

## D-102 — Neighbourhood maps on Google Maps (Jared, 2026-11-04)
- **Decision:** Google Maps JavaScript API, to match the look of the maps Jared likes. Jared accepts the cost: 10,000 map loads a month are free and Google charges US$7 per 1,000 after that (Google's pricing page, updated 2026-11-02). MapLibre with self-hosted tiles stays the fallback if the bill ever matters.
- **Built (engine only, no real outlines yet):** `src/components/AreaMap.astro` (hub mode: every area filled, outlined and labelled, popup "<Area> - <Region>" with a link; area mode: only the page's own area in a strong outline, neighbours not drawn, per Jared's reading of her profile map), `src/lib/areamap.js`, data format `src/data/maps/<region>.json`, block type `areamap` in `GenericPage.astro`, 4 tests in `tests/areamap.test.mjs`. The map loads only when scrolled into view, uses cooperative gestures on touch, and a list of area links under the hub map is the keyboard and screen-reader equivalent. Grouped areas say "MLS® sub-area group" in the popup.
- **Not verified:** the Google calls were exercised only against a stub because this environment cannot reach Google. The first real render needs the key (`docs/google-maps-key-setup.md`) and a look on Jared's preview.
- **Outlines:** our own, drawn from boundary streets and public data (never traced or copied from another site), each `draft` until Jared confirms it. Waiting on: `coordinates.csv`, the boundary streets for the nine South Surrey and White Rock areas (or a board file with its terms), and which board covers South Surrey and White Rock.
- **Not in scope yet:** area-page stats blocks use our past-year sold figures, not live "Active Listings Summary" (live feed stays on MyRealPage); HPI chart only after the board's terms are checked; census pages are phase 2.

## D-103 — Four Google features on top of the map engine (Jared approved 2026-11-04)
- **Sold-number shading (hub map):** areas that name a `sold` key (a sub-area in `sold-12m.json`) are shaded by our own closed-sales figures. Property type (houses, condos, townhomes) and measure (median price, price per sq ft, days on market) are switchable. Shading is a cream-to-black ramp by rank (readable in greyscale), five groups with about the same number of areas each. A type with fewer than 5 closed sales in an area is left grey. A text table of the same figures sits under the map (`<details>`), and the map popup and labels repeat the value. Medians cannot be combined across sub-areas, so a grouped area needs its own sold figure or stays grey. Built in `src/lib/choropleth.js`, `src/components/AreaMap.astro`; 5 tests.
- **Address suggestions:** `src/lib/address-autocomplete.js` adds a plain accessible combobox to address inputs (Places API (New) autocomplete, Canada only, biased to the Fraser Valley, 4+ characters, 300 ms delay, five suggestions). Google loads only when the field is first focused. Suggestions are never stored, and the required "Google Maps" text attribution shows under the list (Google's Places policy page; text form used because the brand rule is no Google logos and this is attribution, not an endorsement claim; Jared can swap in the logo if he prefers). The forms are still the inert preview stubs (BL-05), so nothing shows yet; the Worker adds an optional "Address check" line from the Address Validation API when the secret `GOOGLE_ADDRESS_VALIDATION_KEY` exists, never blocking a lead (`checkAddress` in `src/worker/forms.js`, tested).
- **Drive time to your work (area pages):** `src/components/CommuteTime.astro`, block type `commute`. The visitor types an address; Google's Routes library (browser, traffic-aware) gives an estimate to the centre of the area's outline. Nothing is stored or sent to us, and results are shown, not saved, because Google limits storing. Leave times: now, weekday 8:00 a.m., weekday 5:00 p.m., worked out in Pacific time (`src/lib/commute.js`, tested). The note says the time is to the middle of the neighbourhood.
- **3D flyover:** every hub map gets a "View 3D flyover" button by default (camera framed from the region's bounding box; a region file can tune it with `flyover:{...}` or turn it off with `flyover:false`); nothing 3D loads until it is pressed. The 3D view needs only the Maps JavaScript API, which is on. Coverage in the Fraser Valley is unverified, so test each region on the preview and set `flyover:false` where it looks empty.
- **Shared loader:** `src/lib/gmaps.js` loads Google once for all of these.
- **Verified:** all of it against a stand-in for Google in a headless browser (shading, switching, popup, 3D toggle, suggestions, keyboard, drive time request); not against real Google (no network from here). First real check is on the private preview.
- **Not built:** Solar (Canadian coverage unconfirmed), live Places search (US$32 per 1,000 after 5,000 free; our own schools data covers it), pre-printed drive times (Google limits storing).
- **Setup done in the console:** see `docs/google-maps-key-setup.md`.
- **Privacy page:** needs a line that maps, address suggestions and drive time load from Google in the visitor's browser. Wording draft: "Some pages load Google Maps in your browser to show a map, suggest addresses as you type, or estimate a drive time. That sends the page address and what you type into those fields to Google. We do not receive or store it. See Google's privacy policy."
- **Draft outlines cannot ship (D-103 addendum):** `draftsBlockLaunch` makes a launch build (`PUBLIC_PREVIEW=false`) fail while any area on a map is still `draft`. Jared confirms each outline before it is switched to `confirmed`. Nine South Surrey/White Rock boundary streets were supplied by Jared from Paragon's "FVREB Sub Areas" layer on 2026-11-04; the outlines are not built yet (exact street coordinates need OpenStreetMap or city open data, which this environment and Jared's Chrome extension could not open). Board terms for showing the outlines publicly are still to be confirmed with FVREB.

### D-104 South Surrey / White Rock draft outlines (2026-11-04)
- **What:** `src/data/maps/south-surrey-white-rock.json` holds the nine MLS sub-areas as our own drawn outlines (all `draft`). Built by `tools/build_ssw_outlines.py` from the boundary streets Jared read off Paragon's FVREB Sub Areas layer; `tools/apply_maps.py` puts the hub map on `/south-surrey-neighbourhoods.html` and each area's own map plus the drive-time box on its page (blocks carry `gen: areamap`, rebuilt on every run).
- **How the lines were placed:** Overpass (OpenStreetMap) refused every request from Jared's Chrome with 406, so no OSM data is used and no OSM credit is needed. The street grid was fitted to seven intersection coordinates looked up on Google Maps (each landed within about 30 m of the fitted grid). King George Blvd, the shoreline and the border are hand-placed from a few named places, so they are good to roughly a block or two, not survey grade.
- **Checks:** all nine outlines are valid shapes, none overlap, no gaps between them (union is one piece, about 85 km2). White Rock comes out at 5.6 km2.
- **Edges Jared flagged as unclear (check these first):** Crescent Beach east edge (drawn at 128 St north of 20 Ave, 136 St south of it); King George Corridor east edge (drawn as a strip 0.005 degrees wide around the highway); Grandview west edge (the same highway edge); Elgin Chantrell's northwest diagonal into Mud Bay (drawn as a straight line from 128 St below Crescent Rd to 40 Ave at 136 St).
- **Launch guard:** a launch build still fails while any area is `draft`. `tests/inventory.test.mjs` sets the test-only variable `TEST_ALLOW_DRAFT_OUTLINES=yes` so its robots check can build in launch mode. That variable must never be set on Cloudflare.
- **Still open:** board terms for showing sub-area outlines publicly (FVREB email draft with Jared); Jared's confirmation of each outline.
- **D-104 update (same day):** King George Blvd now follows Google Maps intersection points at 40, 32, 28, 24, 20, 16 and 8 Ave plus the Peace Arch crossing (8 Ave sits about 330 m east of the first guess). Elgin Chantrell and King George Corridor now meet exactly at 152 St and 28 Ave. Still no overlaps and no gaps. The 36 Ave and 12 Ave lookups returned the wrong places and were not used.
- **D-104 update 2 (same day, visual check):** With Jared logged in, I looked at AutoProp's "Sub Areas" layer in his Chrome as an on-screen reference only (nothing exported or copied; its data stays under AutoProp/board terms). It confirmed Crescent Beach's east edge (128 St to 20 Ave, then 136 St), the White Rock box (16 Ave, 136 St, 160 St), Sunnyside's 28 Ave and 140 St/136 St edges, and the 152 St line for King George Corridor. It also showed that the "highway" on the east side of KGC, Morgan Creek, Grandview and Pacific Douglas is the Highway 99 freeway, not old King George Blvd (99A), which is what the earlier points followed. Fixed: those edges now follow the east side of Highway 99 (read off the screen, about +/-0.003 degrees); Elgin Chantrell and the west side of KGC still follow old King George Blvd. Still draft; the Highway 99 line and the Elgin northwest corner are the least certain.

### D-105 Draft outlines for Surrey, Cloverdale, Langley, Delta, Maple Ridge, Pitt Meadows and Abbotsford (2026-11-04)
- **Decision from Jared:** "do 1, 2 and 3, we dont need FVREB, we are good to go." So no board contact is needed for showing our own drawn outlines, and the FVREB email draft is dropped. All areas stay `draft` until Jared confirms each one; the launch guard in D-104 still blocks a launch build while any are draft.
- **What:** `src/data/maps/{surrey,cloverdale,langley,delta,maple-ridge,pitt-meadows,abbotsford}.json`, built by `tools/build_outlines.py` (helpers in `tools/outline_lib.py`), 64 areas: Surrey 15 (central Surrey and North Surrey together under the Surrey hub), Cloverdale 3, Langley 11, Delta 9, Maple Ridge 9, Pitt Meadows 4, Abbotsford 5 (West, Central, Aberdeen, Poplar, East; added from the second zip, calibrated on Google Maps landmarks, the Hwy 1 / Fraser River line and the Canada-US border). `tools/apply_maps.py` puts the hub map on each hub page and the area map plus drive-time box on each area page. Pages that are hand-built (`/fort-langley.html`) get a hub outline only.
- **How the lines were placed:** corners read off Jared's Paragon "FVREB Sub Areas" screenshots (Paragon-Sub-Area-Boundaries.zip) and his boundary table, placed on the street grid fitted to Google Maps intersection points (latitude = 49.001776 + 0.0018276 x avenue, longitude = -123.220335 + 0.0027568 x street; rms about 25 m). The grid carries on through Langley, Maple Ridge and Pitt Meadows. Ladner was fitted to the Google coordinates of five landmarks (Boundary Bay Airport, Ladner Leisure Centre, Watershed Park, OWL, Brunswick Point). No OpenStreetMap data and no AutoProp data is used. Straight street edges are good to about 70 m; diagonals, Highway 1, river banks and coast are good to about a block or two.
- **Checks:** every outline is a valid shape, none overlap, no gaps inside each region (Shapely). Langley City comes out at 10.3 km2 against about 10.2 km2 for the municipality; Maple Ridge City Hall, Pitt Meadows airport and Pitt Meadows City Hall fall in the expected areas.
- **Where the screenshot disagreed with the boundary table (screenshot used):** Brookswood runs south to about 16 Ave (table said 28 Ave) with its east edge at 216 St above 28 Ave and 208 St below it; Murrayville's west edge is about 211 St (table said 208); Fort Langley's east edge is 240 St and County Line Glen Valley starts there (table said 248); Cloverdale has a wedge down to 196 St at about 44 Ave and Serpentine reaches 32 Ave east of 168 St; Whalley is drawn as the screenshot shows it, not as the table words it.
- **Edges to check first:** Highway 1 (drawn as a straight line from 94.7 Ave at 196 St to 46.7 Ave at 276 St), Fraser River south bank (Edgewater Bar and Fort Langley wharf checked on Google Maps, the rest by eye), Glover Rd diagonal between Willoughby Heights and Salmon River, Langley City's east bump and its south-east step, the Salmon River / Otter District diagonal, Sullivan Station / Cloverdale boundary, Bridgeview, Fraser Heights east edge, Sunshine Hills (south end at the Highway 99 / 91 junction), Neilsen Grove's coast, Albion and Thornhill's southern edge along the river.
- **Not drawn, and why:** Mission, Chilliwack, Sardis, Yarrow, Agassiz, Harrison and East Chilliwack (the screenshots show them only at overview scale, the polygons are large and irregular and run off the screen); Maple Ridge North Meadows, Silver Valley, Websters Corners and Northeast (they run north off the top of the screenshots); East Delta, Ladner Rural and Port Guichon (large coast and river polygons with islands); all eight Tsawwassen areas (edges unclear and cut off at the bottom of the screenshots). These need a closer screenshot of each area, or Jared's boundary streets, before an outline is honest. Hub pages still render with the areas that are drawn.
- **Abbotsford not drawn:** Bradner, Matsqui, Sumas Prairie and Sumas Mountain (cut off at the top or right of the screenshots). The second zip also covered Richmond, Vancouver, Burnaby, New Westminster and Coquitlam, but those layers are letter codes only with no confirmed names and we have no hub pages for them, so they were not attempted. It added no closer images for Mission, Chilliwack or Tsawwassen.
- **AutoProp:** Jared asked whether AutoProp could fill the missing areas. Viewing its Sub Areas layer on screen needs `dashboard.autoprop.ca` allowed in the Claude in Chrome extension (denied so far). If allowed, it is used for on-screen reading only, no export, and any outline stays draft.
- **Unconfirmed names:** the Delta, Tsawwassen, Maple Ridge and Pitt Meadows polygons were labelled on Paragon with letter codes. The decoded names match our sold-figure and page names one for one (for example the 12 Maple Ridge codes and the 12 sub-areas in our data), but Jared should still confirm them when he checks each outline.
- **Still open:** Jared's confirmation of each outline; closer screenshots for the areas not drawn; privacy-page wording for Google (D-103); private preview with the Google key; launch and DNS only with explicit approval.

### D-106 Neighbourhood hubs for Richmond, Vancouver, Burnaby, New Westminster and Coquitlam; 27 new neighbourhood pages (2026-11-05)
- **Request from Jared:** "Please create hubs and pages for Richmond, Vancouver, Burnaby, New Westminster and Coquitlam", then "keep going and keep building". All pages are draft until he approves them.
- **Names:** the five cities' MLS® sub-area names are confirmed by the sub-area column of Jared's Paragon closed-sales export (Richmond 29, Vancouver West and East 49, Burnaby 35, New Westminster 12, Coquitlam 23). The letter and number codes on the Paragon map layer are only needed to draw outlines, not to name pages.
- **Hubs (5):** `/richmond-`, `/vancouver-`, `/burnaby-`, `/new-westminster-` and `/coquitlam-neighbourhood-guides.html`, built by `tools/build_gvr_pages.py`. Each shows every sub-area of the city with what sold in it (houses, condos, townhomes; counts and medians only where 5 or more sold), links to the page for each, a FAQ computed from the same figures, and the city's other pages. Burnaby and Vancouver are split by board area (North, South, East; West side, East side). Each city's homes page and market-stats page links to its hub, every neighbourhood page in the city links to the hub and eight neighbours, and each hub is the first item under its city in the Communities menu.
- **New pages (27):** an MLS® sub-area gets a page when it has no page on the site and 10 or more closed sales in the window. Richmond (14): Brighouse South, Broadmoor, Garden City, Hamilton, Ironwood, Lackner, McLennan North, McNair, Saunders, Steveston North, Steveston South, Steveston Village, Westwind, Woodwards. Vancouver (13): Hastings Sunrise, Marpole, Point Grey, Quilchena, Renfrew, Renfrew Heights, South Marine, South Vancouver, Southlands, Strathcona, Victoria, West End, Yaletown. Burnaby, New Westminster and Coquitlam already had a page for every sub-area with sales (Metrotown's is `metrotown-homes-for-sale`; Westwood Summit had 5 sales and has none). Not built for too few sales: East Richmond (4), Gilmore (2), McLennan (3), Sea Island (8).
- **What a new page holds:** sold figures from the Paragon aggregate only (mix by home type, quarterly medians where each quarter has 5 or more, building tables, the typical home, rank against the rest of the city), a FAQ from the same figures, and local facts from `docs/research/local-notes-gvr/<slug>.md`. Each fact in those notes carries a source URL (City of Richmond, City of Vancouver, SD38, VSB, TransLink, Province); lines marked UNVERIFIED in the notes are not published. The researchers could not verify any MLS sub-area boundary against a City source, so no page states street boundaries.
- **Things to check before launch:** (1) the research was done with a fetch tool that returns summaries, so key figures (Richmond dike heights and design flood, Vancouver Empty Homes Tax rate and dates) should be spot-checked against the sources; (2) Woodwards is not a City place name, so its page has no location copy; (3) Quilchena (Vancouver) is a former golf course, now a park; the note says so; (4) several Vancouver items are dated (2011 to 2016 plan statistics, a 1988 horse count in Southlands), and the notes give the year; (5) no page has a listing feed yet, each needs a MyRealPage search; (6) uniqueness (`tools/uniqueness.py`): 23 of the 27 pages are 0.70 or higher (0.72 to 0.81). Four Richmond pages are just under because no source ties the MLS name to streets or a City planning area: Saunders 0.65, Lackner 0.66, Westwind 0.69, McNair 0.70 (0.697). They need Jared's own notes or the Paragon boundary streets before launch; adding more table rows from the same figures made them worse, so none was added.
- **Code:** table cells can now be links (`{t, h}`), used by the hubs; `tools/uniqueness.py` reads them. `tools/apply_maps.py` knows the five hubs and will add the map when `src/data/maps/<region>.json` exists.
- **Not done yet:** draft map outlines for the five cities (Chrome extension disconnected; needs Google Maps landmark calibration); numbered Richmond areas on the Paragon layer cannot be matched to names with certainty, so those outlines will be flagged for Jared to confirm.

### D-108 Heading lettering: Montserrat 800, tight, uppercase for short headings (2026-11-05)
- **Direction (Jared, 2026-11-05):** take only the lettering from the Varing Group site (heavy, tightly spaced uppercase headings with wide-tracked small labels); no colour or brand changes. Previewed on the home page, approved ("its a good font"), then applied sitewide.
- **Rule:** every H1–H4 is Montserrat 800 with -.02em tracking. H1 and H2 of 6 words or fewer and 40 characters or fewer also get uppercase (`caps` class from `src/lib/caps.ts`, used by GenericPage, ListingPage, ReviewCards and the static pages); longer ones stay in normal case at the same weight. Eyebrow labels are Montserrat 600 at .24em. Body (Lato), colours, layout and all heading text are unchanged, so titles, H1s and SEO are unaffected.
- **Font:** one added file, `public/fonts/montserrat-latin-800-normal.woff2` (self-hosted, same family as before, so no new font approval). The Varing site uses a paid font (bentonSans); Montserrat is the free match.
- **Not done:** a light (300) weight for two-weight headings; none used.

### D-109 "Show nearby places" buttons on the neighbourhood maps (Jared, 2026-11-05)
- **Decision:** Jared asked for the opt-in nearby-places button after a review of three Google APIs. Built on every map (72 pages): three toggle buttons, Parks, Cafés and Groceries, shown only after the map has loaded. Nothing is requested until a button is pressed.
- **How it works:** one Places API (New) `searchNearby` call per press, around the middle of the visible map, radius half the shorter side of the view (500 m to 5 km), up to 20 results, nearest first. Pins are lettered P, C and G (not colour alone), the same places are listed as text under the map, and a note states the search radius. Pressing a pressed button clears its pins; turning it off and on again re-searches the current view.
- **Rules kept:** fields are `displayName` and `location` only (no ratings, hours or photos: lowest billing tier and our no-ratings rule, D-85 addendum); results live in page memory only (Google limits storing); the places show on a Google map with Google's logo, plus the note "Places data from Google Maps, shown live and not stored"; 3D flyover hides the buttons. The existing privacy-page line (D-103) covers it, since the view's centre point goes to Google.
- **Cost:** about US$32 per 1,000 requests after 5,000 free a month for Nearby Search at this level (figure from D-103; confirm on Google's pricing page). The US$25 budget alert in the console covers it; optional daily quota in `docs/google-maps-key-setup.md`.
- **Other two Google APIs reviewed, not added:** Aerial View API supports US postal addresses only (Google's FAQ), so it cannot cover Canada. Address Validation already exists as the optional non-blocking "Address check" on form leads (D-103); Canada is supported.
- **Code:** `src/lib/nearby-places.js` (helpers), `AreaMap.astro`, `tests/nearby-places.test.mjs` (6 tests), `tools/qa_nearby_places.py` (headless check against a Google stand-in; found and fixed a LatLng `this` bug). Not yet run against real Google; first real check is on the private preview.

### D-110 Brand map style, and three more Google options reviewed (Jared, 2026-11-05)
- **Built: brand map style** (`src/lib/map-style.js`, applied in `AreaMap.astro`): cream and white land, white roads with grey outlines, grey highways, parks a touch darker with their names kept, water light grey, grey labels. Google's shop and restaurant pins and its neighbourhood labels are off (its neighbourhood names are not our MLS areas; our own labels and the nearby-places buttons replace them); transit stations stay. Black, white, cream and grey only (a test enforces it). It uses the Maps JavaScript API `styles` option, so there is no console set-up; the satellite view is unchanged. Not seen on real Google tiles yet (no network from here): check the colours of the area shading against the new base on the private preview. If Jared wants to tweak it visually later, the same style can move to a Cloud Map ID in the console.
- **Not built: Datasets API.** Our maps hold 65 areas across 8 regions, at most 15 shapes on one map, which a browser draws instantly; the Datasets API would also need the boundaries uploaded to Google and our sold-figure shading re-done. Revisit only if a single map ever carries hundreds of shapes.
- **Not built: Photorealistic 3D Tiles (Map Tiles API).** The "View 3D flyover" button (D-103) already uses Google's photorealistic 3D from the Maps JavaScript API. Coverage of the Fraser Valley is not confirmed (Google's page only says 3D surface data is limited to the areas on its coverage map): check the preview, set `flyover:false` where it looks empty, and tune the camera per region with `flyover:{...}`. A hand-built 3D viewer on the raw tiles would add weight and maintenance for no more than the button gives.

### D-113 Eight pages raised over 0.70 uniqueness; research file paths removed (Jared, 2026-11-05)
- **Pages:** Saunders, Lackner, Westwind and McNair (Richmond), Anmore, Harbour Chines, Mission townhomes under $1.2M and Ladner townhomes. Before: 0.65 to 0.70. After: 0.74 to 0.84. Every new page is now at 0.70 or above (617 new pages, none under).
- **How:** `tools/build_uplift.py` (gen `uplift`) writes a section and two FAQ entries per page from the Paragon aggregate only: rank among the neighbouring sub-areas, the nearest areas above and below on price, what the median implies (lot size, build year, a rough house size that the text labels as rough), the spread of the middle half, quarterly medians where each quarter has 5+ sales, strata fees as a yearly figure and a share of price, and comparison with the city or district figure. Mission and Ladner also get a price-band table. No outside facts were added and no median is published on fewer than 5 sales. Rerun after `build_sold_layer.py`.
- **Mission finding:** all 65 Mission townhomes that sold in the window closed under $1,000,000, so a "$1.2M" ceiling covers the whole townhome market. The text says so.
- **File paths removed:** 37 local layers carried internal research file paths and read-date notes in FAQ answers ("(BC Ministry K-12 file, ~/workspace/..., read 2026-11-01)"). `tools/strip_file_paths.py` removed 39 of them, keeping the source name where there was one; a new test fails if a path reaches the layers again.
- **Not changed:** phrases such as "no locator run is on record" (about 66 pages) read as plain caution to a buyer and were left; "in this pass" wording on `chilliwack-homes-over-1-8m` is listed for Jared's review.

- **Names:** Paragon's Sub-Area/Community lookup lists 23 Coquitlam codes (VCQBM Burke Mountain, CY Canyon Springs, CH Cape Horn, CC Central Coquitlam, CS Chineside, CE Coquitlam East, CW Coquitlam West, ER Eagle Ridge CQ, HC Harbour Chines, HP Harbour Place, HO Hockaday, ML Maillardville, MB Meadow Brook, NH New Horizons, NC North Coquitlam, PR Park Ridge Estates, RP Ranch Park, RS River Springs, SC Scott Creek, SV Summitt View, UE Upper Eagle Ridge, WP Westwood Plateau, WS Westwood Summit CQ). The layer shows the last two letters once its "Show Labels" toggle is switched on. Correction to the earlier boundary notes: WA, GL, OH, LP, BL and RD are Port Coquitlam areas, not Coquitlam.
- **What:** `src/data/maps/coquitlam.json`, built by `tools/build_coquitlam_outlines.py`: Westwood Plateau, Hockaday, Summitt View, Upper Eagle Ridge, Scott Creek, Canyon Springs, North Coquitlam, New Horizons, River Springs, Park Ridge Estates, Burke Mountain, Eagle Ridge, Ranch Park, Meadow Brook, Chineside, Harbour Place and Harbour Chines. Read by eye from two Paragon screenshots (the second registered to the first by feature matching), placed with a Web Mercator fit to seven Google Maps landmark coordinates (misses them by 66 m at worst). Overlays of the outlines on both screenshots sit on the Paragon boundaries.

Whether the two blue "CE" polygons (one under Ranch Park, one across Port Coquitlam) are one area or two is also unresolved until I can look again.

Lehman, Real Estate Lawyer & Notary Fees, and the six BC buying-rules articles (acreage, condo, duplex, land, mobile home, townhome). **D-119 (2026-11-05): thin-search note on 24 pages.** The 24 neighbourhood searches that had under 5 listings when built (4 area, 9 houses, 11 condos and townhomes) keep their live widget; a widget is not hidden (a static page cannot know the live count, and hiding after load shifts the layout) and none is folded into a neighbouring area (the page title, sold figures and prices are for that sub-area alone). `tools/add_thin_search_notes.py` (gen `thin`, idempotent, run after the other generators) adds, directly under the widget, one short sentence saying the list may be short and offering a saved search ("Contact me", `/contact.html`), plus links to the city homes page and the city neighbourhood map where one exists (Port Moody and Port Coquitlam have no map page yet, so homes link only). There is no separate alert sign-up page on the site; a saved search is set up by Jared on request. Three wordings, chosen by slug. First attempts lowered Burnaby Hospital below the 0.70 uniqueness gate; the final short wording keeps all 617 new pages at 0.70 or above. Build 1,674 pages; 99 tests pass.

**D-121 (2026-11-05): outlines from Jared's Paragon close-up pack (draft), part 1.** Drawn from the screenshots in `Paragon-Sub-Area-Boundaries-with-Closeups.zip`, each fixed to the ground with landmark coordinates, one builder per area group (all `draft`; D-104 still blocks a launch build while any is draft). Google Maps began showing CAPTCHAs part-way through, so some landmark fits use OpenStreetMap place coordinates instead (Coquitlam, Burnaby frames C/D and the Lougheed frame; misses 6 to 43 m); Mission and the Abbotsford outer areas are chained to the existing Chilliwack and Abbotsford fits.

Mount Pleasant is split at Ontario St, not Main St; both halves share one page, so ids are `mount-pleasant-vw` / `mount-pleasant-ve` and `apply_maps.py` now writes one map block per page that shows both (AreaMap and CommuteTime accept comma-joined ids).
